“...How coincidental. I tested it with Aegis Barrier, and it really resonated.”

“So it’s a true set item with the shield?”

“Yes. Both Aegis Dragon Armor and Aegis Barrier... are resonance relics.”

Resonance relics mean set items that grant extra effects when equipped together.

For example, No. 7611 ‘Necromancer’s Deception’ and No. 7612 ‘Necromancer’s Arrogance’ skull belts provide a resonance effect.

“When either activates, an additional 50% HP recovery triggers.”

Anyway, learning it was a set updated existing info.

“So what’s the resonance effect between the armor and shield?”

“Hmm... looks like the regeneration effect itself is the resonance effect.”

“All increased regen options are part of the Aegis set resonance.”

“Oh, so it has a passive effect too?”

“Yes... appears to have one passive and one active ability.”

Meaning the armor has one passive and one active option...

The active was quickly figured out through experiments.

「Jurven Havelion cast 5th-grade attack magic [Lightning Spear].」

The effect was simple.

「Jurven Havelion cast 4th-grade attack magic [Storm Rush].」

Being armor, it was defense-related.

「Jurven Havelion cast 4th-grade attack magic [Ice Sword]...」

Well, they say the best defense is offense, right?

「Reflects accumulated damage.」

The active effect was somewhat like the essence of a gacha capsule toy.

The ‘counterattack’ mechanism.

And...

Swishhh!

Like a one-time-use skeleton summoned for counterattack, a sort of summoned spirit appeared behind me.

These two features were like the gacha toy.

Far more intimidating in scale than a mere skeleton.

A giant Viking warrior spirit wielded a greatsword.

Crackling sound!

Clang!

Ah, that’s why I was planning to study it alone later.

Uh... yeah...

To sum up, that’s roughly it...

No words.

Because I agreed.

Depending on the passive, this might be a single Numbers-level item at least.

Ibaekho’s remark just went in one ear and out the other as I resumed research.

But...

One day, two days, three days...

No matter how much time passed, the armor’s passive remained a mystery.

“If it’s not some special or hidden effect, we can only find out if monsters appear.”

Ibaekho’s deflated mood spread to everyone.

This was when a leader had to not show weakness.

I encouraged them, and we resumed thorough searching of the Golden Ruins.

Chapter 691: Mystery (5)

I know the power of this ‘Record’ better than anyone, because I experienced it once. My heart thumped recalling the past I went to through the Fragments of Records.

Back then, I caused chaos trying to change the future where someone died, but failed. The stories written in the Record Stone always come true.

Of course, some like Amelia used tricks of ‘deception’...

‘This time, such tricks won’t work.’

How could I even try? I’m no naive ten-year-old. Faking death to fool my comrades was impossible. Besides, I don’t even know when it happened. My heart thumped again... three people... ‘three...’

In Dungeon & Stone, comrades dying is common. But why does this small number feel so huge and despairing? Heart thumping.

While my heart tightened, my head cooled, thinking. Who were those three? Or rather, who should they be?

As the leader of a group, even the unthinkable thoughts swiftly passed.

“Knowing the ending is truly a painful thing.”

Auril Gavis, who gave me the greatest worry of my life, nodded as if understanding me. Seeing that made blood surge to my head.

“...What?”

I wanted to punch that old face right then and there. But...

“......”

I had to hold back. Throwing a punch here would only be to my own loss.

“So... when...”

Clenching my teeth until my body trembled, I asked him.

“Do you know when... the events written here will happen?”

“I don’t know. If the Record Stone was intact, maybe. But with just these fragments, it’s hard to estimate.”

‘So, there’s nothing here that can help me?’

At that moment, something like a thread snapped in my mind, and Auril Gavis continued.

“But judging how records are written, this must also be one event.”

In other words, it’s not that one person dies after another over time, but that three die during one ‘incident.’

I gritted my teeth and asked.

“...Do you know who those three are?”

This was a question I debated hundreds or thousands of times whether to ask. But as if he knew my thoughts, the old man answered easily.

“I don’t know.”

No excuses, no explanations. Just “I don’t know.” Somehow, that made me both angry and relieved. If those names were known, I would have gone mad.

“You seem conflicted. Are your comrades that important to you?”

“...Don’t poke me. I’m really at my limit.”

“Then so be it.”

Showing superhuman patience, Auril Gavis said no more, and silence stretched out.

My heart still pounded even after some time passed, and the strength in my toes wouldn’t release. But...

‘Information.’

Somewhat my reason returned.

“Elder.”

“Looks like you’re coming to your senses.”

After all, what is recorded is something I can’t immediately change.

“Why did you show me this?”

I judged this was the first thing to find out. This old man, who specializes in toying with people, wouldn’t have shown it without reason.

“Reason... Honestly, it’s close to whimsy. If you had left as you were and not asked where this place was, I wouldn’t have shown it.”

“......”

“But you look like you don’t believe me?”

Of course I wouldn’t. Even if I hadn’t left, I’d suspect some dark scheme behind this. But...

“You’re clearly the former.”

“...?”

“You said you preferred the latter earlier, but that was only because you wanted to be that person. You are actually the former.”

At first, I didn’t understand, but soon it clicked. ~Nоvеlight~ It was a continuation of the Zen koan: a lie you want to believe and a truth you want to disbelieve.

“I hope my word about whimsy is a lie, but it’s certainly true.”

Though it sounded like a forced logic to confuse the point, I had no sharp rebuttal. Yet I still looked for distance.

“Still, I don’t disappoint you. If you ask a hundred people, all will choose ‘truth,’ but in reality, when faced with the situation, it reverses...”

“......”

“Isn’t that right, Bjorn Yandel... or rather, Lee Hansoo?”

Damn, no words. I gave up trying to win this old man with words.

“...Alright. I only started this talk for no reason. I wasn’t aiming for anything.”

Saying it resignedly, Auril Gavis gazed sadly at the Record Fragments scattered like a galaxy in midair.

“I don’t know how you see me, but I’m no monster. And as Baekho said, I’m not omnipotent...”

“......”

“I’m just an ordinary human, struggling and suffering hard for one thing. Just like you.”

Well, sorry to be blunt, but no matter how much he says that, he just looks like a crazy old man to me. No need to ruin the mood.

“...Since I’m here, can I see something else too?”

“Your comrades must be waiting outside. Is that alright?”

“...Comrades?”

They’re just ‘companions’ enough.

“It’s fine, don’t worry about it.”

Chapter 698

“Hmm, in that case, I’ll show you just one more. But what to show... Ah, this time, why don’t you pick one yourself?”

Auril Gavis’s suggestion wasn’t bad. Unlike before, he didn’t pick one for me but let me choose, which meant I could avoid any trickery.

“......”

I hesitated for a long time, and seeing my frustration, he added beside me:

“What’s there to worry about? No matter what you choose, it’ll be your fate anyway.”

“If that’s so, then why breathe or think at all?”

Though I snapped back, after hearing him, I felt it pointless to agonize over such things. So...

“That one there. Give me that solid, inverted-triangle-shaped one.”

I picked the biggest fragment I could see, thinking the larger it was, the more it would contain.

I wondered if he memorized all the fragments’ records.

“Hmm... of all things, you chose that one.”

“Why? Is there a problem?”

“No, not a problem exactly... but, well, if this is your fate, it’s fate.”

Auril Gavis asked again if I really wanted to see it, and after a moment of thought, I said yes. Whoosh!

The fragment slowly descended toward his outstretched hand. Soon it was close enough to see the text.

But had I made a wrong choice? ‘Why are there so many empty spots?’ Though it looked biggest, many parts were broken or worn, so only a few letters were legible.

Still, reading the intact parts gave:

“[...On the day Noni burned, this... demon spirit ‘———’ was betrayed by trusted comrades...]”

The symbol that represented the name matched what I’d seen earlier.

So ‘———’ meant me...

“On the day Hwangdo Karnon burned... betrayed by trusted comrades...?”

Filling the unreadable parts by context, that was the gist.

“Did I interpret that correctly?”

“Interpretation is always up to the reader at any moment.”

“Ha, tsk...”

Had I been foolish to say I’d look? My already complicated head grew more so. Some accident caused three comrades to die.

Whether after or before, one comrade betrayed me. Knowing an unchangeable future is a curse, not a blessing.

“Interesting.”

“...Are you enjoying my suffering?”

“More than that, it’s strange that among so many fragments, you chose the one with you in it. That’s the power of fate, I suppose.”

Auril Gavis’s “interesting” wasn’t empty praise; he even asked if I wanted to see one more fragment.

But...

“No, I’m good. I don’t want my head to hurt more.”

“Hmm, if you say so, so be it. I won’t /N_o_v_e_l_i_g_h_t/ offer more.”

Thus the Record Stone tour ended, and we descended the stairs back to the cave quietly.

“So, shall we leave now?”

“Yes, even leaving late like this, Ibaekho will make a fuss.”

“That guy’s like you, wanting to know everything.”

“Weren’t you the one telling me not to be close to him?”

“That advice still stands. Being close to Ibaekho brings no good.”

“Is that from your perspective, or the elder’s?”

“Both.”

Talking with this old man for a long time felt pointless again. I never knew if what he said was true or false, making it worse.

Though many words were exchanged, nothing felt trustworthy.

“Since we’re on the topic, one more thing.”

“Go ahead.”

“Be careful with Ibaekho.”

“...Didn’t you say that before?”

I tilted my head, and Auril Gavis continued as if to make sure I heard it.

“I said he’s a supporting role, but Ibaekho is a very unusual existence. See those?”

He pointed at the hundreds of fragments slowly orbiting the air.

“I’ve seen many records here, but none mention Ibaekho.”

“...?”

“That’s very strange. Someone with so much influence and closely connected to you not appearing in any record.”

If that’s true, it’s definitely odd. Could Ibaekho be hiding a secret we don’t know?

I don’t know, but Auril Gavis said again:

“So be cautious of Ibaekho. Not appearing in the ‘records’ means he is freer from them. He’s not without influence, but at least less than us.”

“Don’t worry too much; he’s already on alert.”

“That’s good.”

“We’ll meet again someday, won’t we?”

“That’s fate.”

“Well then, I’ll be going. Let’s hope it’s not too late next time.”

“I hope so too.”

With that, Auril Gavis watched me silently. I didn’t say anything either, just glanced him up and down once more, nodded, and stepped into the portal.

And then...

“Oh! You’re back!”

“Thank goodness! I was worried!”

“What? Why so late? Baron! You talked to that old man, didn’t you? Huh?”

Opening my eyes after the portal, I heard many voices all around. Ibaekho, Leg Auress, Jaina Flyer, Belvev Ruin Genesis, Yulben Havelion.

The fact that all but me were demon spirits was one thing. Including me, the group totaled six.

“What’s with that look? I just felt a chill.”

Ignoring Ibaekho’s fuss, I recalled the record I’d read.

Suddenly, a thought struck me like lightning.

[A demon spirit from another world ‘———’ lost three comrades and only then realized the path he must take.]

The three unnamed comrades.

“You all, become my comrades.”

It might just be a wild idea, but since the record said “three comrades,”

‘If I make them my comrades and travel together every day... maybe those three will die and it will pass.’

It sounds crazy at first, but logically there’s nothing wrong.

Still, whether that will really happen remains a question. But what else can I do? I have to try as far as I can.

“Comrades... huh?”

My suggestion probably sounded confusing. Everyone froze without blinking at my sudden idea. Ibaekho’s expression was priceless.

“Wow, now I’m even more curious. What did you hear from that old man to suddenly say that?”

His stunned look faded, replaced by sharp eyes glaring at me. I realized I’d rushed.

Even with comrades’ lives at stake, I should have proceeded carefully step by step.

“Baron, what did you talk about with that old man?”

“...Not much.”

“Come on, who’d believe that? Nothing happened but you changed like that?”

“We just kept traveling together, and I thought it’d be reassuring if you joined us in the labyrinth.”

Of course, it was a lie. The labyrinth was the one place where you must watch your back the most. How could I go in with these guys?

They hid knives even when facing each other alone. If I joined, it’d be chaos.

“Hmm... but what about your clan if you travel with us?”

Ibaekho probed, clearly not believing my claim of no hidden agenda, but willing to listen for now.

“Well, that’s something to think about later. Besides, you haven’t agreed yet.”

I dodged naturally as if I hadn’t given it much thought. But...

“Oh! Baron Yandel the Giant wants to be our comrade? We really have to think seriously now?”

Ibaekho muttered in a tone like reading a textbook. Obviously mocking me, except for one person.

“Uh... Baekho...? If the Baron joins the team, I might get edged out...”

“Aures... please just stay quiet...”

“W-why should I? My position’s being threatened—!”

Jaina’s quiet attempt to calm him failed, and Ibaekho snapped irritably.

“No, that’s not it! Just be quiet! You wanna get kicked out for real?”

“.......”

“Ah, seriously annoying.”

Ibaekho sighed deeply, deflated by Aures’s inability to read the mood. The textbook tone was long gone.

“Hey, Baron.”

“I’m listening.”

“Do you know what explorers are best at?”

He gave me a look asking what that was. Ibaekho stared and opened his mouth.

“Figuring things out.”

“......?”

“Whether I’m at an advantage or disadvantage, if they’re strong or I’m stronger.”

My heart thumped! Without hearing more, a bad feeling started. As expected, bad feelings never miss.

“You have to know that to do it right.”

“...Know what?”

“Know what you already know.”

After saying that, Ibaekho gestured, and Parmel Harbae, Leg Auress, and Jaina gathered by his side.

And then...

“Hey, are you just gonna stand there?”

He said bluntly to GM.

“If you do, you’ll catch stray fire and die.”

The threat was so intense it sent chills down my spine. GM froze and glanced between Ibaekho and me, tension deepening.

Of course, as ‘human,’ GM didn’t take long to choose. Facing those four with just a tank and a mage was almost impossible.

“S-sorry...”

Still feeling some guilt, GM apologized to me and hurried toward Ibaekho. I didn’t feel betrayed.

After all, that guy and I weren’t really comrades. I silently raised my shield, ready for combat at any moment.

Watching me, Ibaekho said:

“Don’t be too upset. Honestly, I’m more upset.”

“Why’s that?”

“Because it’s obvious you talked to that old man. I don’t know what about, but I have to hear.”

“......”

“Even if I have to use explorer’s methods.”

What does he think explorers are? While thinking that, I realized I agreed with him.

If you’re weak, you’ll end up feeling wronged. That’s just how this world works, like paying money to buy something.

Step by step, Ibaekho approached ready to strike. But he didn’t back down even a step. That’s right.

‘Running away is impossible.’

If I ran, where would I go? With this clumsy body, I’d be caught soon enough.

Maybe my resolve surprised him.

“Wow, you’re not backing down here either?”

Ibaekho muttered, a bit surprised, but I saw no reason to be.

After all, a shield is made to block attacks from the front, not the back.

“Do you think I can’t kill you?”

Well, I hadn’t exactly ruled that out. Ibaekho wants me to open the Abyss Gate. He can never kill me. But...

“What to do? Even if I can’t kill him, I already have dozens of ways to make him miserable.”

That doesn’t mean I’d ignore him. Like he said, there are countless ways to torment me without killing me.

No need to go as far as Briat’s ‘interrogation.’

“For example, take all his gear. Or neuter him so he won’t be distracted by women anymore.”

Ah, um... neutering wasn’t expected.

“......”

Damn, should I just say it all honestly?

“Or threaten to kill all your comrades when we get back—”

“No way.”

“Behe—laaaaaaah!!!”

I shouted to steady my weakening heart.

“What are you barking about?”

Ibaekho is a wild dog. A wild dog that tears apart the weak without leaving a scrap. So...

“Shut up and fight.”

This is no place to back down.***

Maybe he thought talking more here would only make him look like a noisy dog. Ibaekho closed his mouth, and silence fell.

“......”

“......”

Chapter 699

Silence stretched as the standoff continued. Had Ibaekho finished sorting out his thoughts during that time?

“I thought maybe you had some backup to act this aggressively... Well, either way’s fine. We’ll see once it starts.”

“......”

“Let’s start by taking off our Baron’s clothes.”

“......?”

“That way we can neuter him or whatever else.”

With that, Ibaekho’s figure vanished before my eyes.

“......!”

His movement was too fast for my agility to chase. Yet the warrior’s instinct born from countless hardships caught his attack, raising my shield. But...

“PVP? Think that’ll work?”

No impact was felt on the shield where I anticipated the punch’s path.

“Damage blocking only works—”

Unexpectedly, the real impact came from behind.

“Only when you block with your shield, huh?”

KA-BOOM! The shock was like being hit by siege weaponry. Without a chance to cry out, even using [Giant Form], my body was pushed backward.

Then... ‘...Huh?’ As I regained senses, a heavy blow struck my jaw, and my body tilted back again.

What was that? An uppercut? I couldn’t tell how it happened from that angle, but one thing was certain — this time, damage landed properly.

Enough to make me want to vomit everything inside.

“Ugh...”

“Cool, huh? When I hit like this, it feels like damage doubles. Oh, by the way, this is an extra effect not in the game... Hup!”

Trying to regain posture, I swung the Demon Crusher, but Ibaekho jumped with frustrating agility to evade.

“Oh, looks like you’re ready.”

Suddenly, as Ibaekho stepped back to create distance—

“No hard feelings.”

When I looked up dazed, the surroundings glowed red. Slowly raising my head, a huge sphere reminiscent of the sun was falling.

Whooosh! A sphere covered in ominously deep red magic. Unfortunately, it was even bigger than my shield in [Giant Form].

At least ten times larger. ‘Perfect guard is impossible.’ As soon as I made this quick judgment, I raised my shield to block the sky and activated Dragon Mode.

「Character used [Scales of Greed].」

「Character’s magic resistance is over 500.」

「All magic damage taken is reduced by 50%.」

Bellarios’s active skill on the invincible barbarian (final form) that handles ‘magic resistance.’

No sooner had I used the skill, a brilliant light engulfed my entire body, swallowing even the noise. And then... shiiiiiish!

Smoke rose like opening the lid on a boiling pot, from the scales covering my skin. Hot, stinging, burning. But...

“Wow, even after that hit, not a scratch.”

No critical injury was sustained — after all, my shield blocked all vital areas.

“Our Baron really became a monster.”

After briefly marveling at my intact stance, Ibaekho closed the gap again, buzzing attacks like a fly.

Bam! Bam! Heavy jabs fired continuously. KA-BOOM! Interspersed with powerful strikes.

Swoosh! Parmel Harbae’s curse magic and Jaina’s debuffs covered me. Whooosh!

Harbae’s magic, which my shield couldn’t block, struck intermittently. But... bam! Ibaekho, who usually beat me like a dog, tilted his head in confusion.

“What? Your neck won’t even turn now?”

That’s because Shield Barbarian gets tougher the more he’s hit.

「Physical resistance rises proportionally to absorbed magic damage.」

How many magic hits have I taken so far?

「Character’s physical resistance exceeds 750.」

「All physical damage is reduced by half.」

Not only is [Evolved Shell] Stage 3 active...

「Character cast [Iron Fortress].」

「[Evolved Shell] effect increased by 1.5 times.」

I’m about to use that too.

Thunk! Compared to before, the normal attacks felt like mere tickles. This was why I could stay confident even 4:1.

‘They can’t use ultimates here anyway.’ Like [Star’s Demise] or that ‘thing’ used on Briat in the Golden Ruins.

As long as allies are nearby, using those is risky. Conversely, if no allies were around, I could use them without hesitation...

“This should ease your worries. Baron, you ready to go all out? Hey! Everyone, back off!”

In some ways, this resembled how Ibaekho smartly fought by knowing my shield’s traits earlier. ‘Do they think I’m a dumb monster?’

I’d never wait, knowing everything, for him to create distance.

“Behe—laaaaaaaah!!”

With a battle cry, I charged at the rear line. After being pummeled all day, it was time.

“Aures!!”

It’s time to catch at least one. As I rushed in, Leg Auress raised his shield to block.

Tank vs. tank. Normally, they couldn’t deal critical damage to each other, making it a stalemate...

“Don’t worry! I’ll protect Lord Ruin Genesis—.”

What’s he saying? Does he think I’m a pure tank? No. 87 Crowl’s Demon Crusher. No. 687 Siege Slayer. And Ogre’s [Swing].

These are now basically normal attacks since I recently got ultimates. 「Character used [Aegis Dragon Armor].」

After being hit by Ibaekho’s attacks and Harbae’s magic, the damage I’d accumulated: 「Reflecting accumulated damage.」 That’s enough.

No. ???? Aegis Dragon Armor. The active effect is quite flashy. Actually...

‘It always makes my chest swell with pride.’ It has a more ‘overwhelming’ effect than any ability or equipment I have.

Like this: swoooooosh! Behind me, the summoned soul warrior swings a massive greatsword that looks like it could tear down castle walls.

The target: Parmel Harbae, Ruin Genesis. If only the priest Jaina were out of the way, the fight would be much more manageable.

But the problem is the gatekeeper Leg Auress in front. ‘Hmm, not really a problem?’

Indeed, what happened next matched my expectations.

Leg Auress stood with a gigantic shield before Harbae, a thick magical barrier layered dozens deep forming over him.

‘A tank protecting a mage, and a mage casting spells to protect that tank...’

It sounds romantic, but...

‘They’re a pain in the ass.’ Nothing changes no matter what.

No matter how they combined power, how could they stop me? They couldn’t even stop me properly during the experiment. Crackle crackle crackle!

The magical barrier of the top-tier mage tore like paper. One layer, two, three, four... No matter how many layers, it was meaningless.

That ruthless warrior’s greatsword didn’t discriminate at all. Crackle crackle crackle!

Swinging wildly, the greatsword finally shattered the last magical barrier and sent flying even Leg Auress, the final wall.

“Gahk!”

Okay, tank down. Bam! Ibaekho hammered normal attacks from behind, but I could just ignore them.

‘GM is watching...’

Since sacrifices can’t be made outside the walls, even the priest has no real way to stop me.

Now my job is to finish off Parmel Harbae, who has used all his defensive spells...

“...Huh?”

Then an unexpected variable burst out, surprisingly positive for me at first though I didn’t realize it right away.

“Why... won’t it disappear?”

The soul warrior summoned by Aegis Dragon Armor didn’t vanish after striking once. No, it even retracted its greatsword and prepared to strike again. Strangely, those brief moments felt like slow motion.

“Uh...?”

Ibaekho’s stunned face forgot to guard me.

“...!”

Jaina looked worried. GM wide-eyed watching. And...

“This is troubling.”

Parmel Harbae urgently began casting. As my senses flooded with real-time info, I instinctively understood what happened.

[Stacks build as you take hits, and physical damage scales with stacks.]

Due to experimental limits, we’d only seen up to Stage 4 of Aegis Dragon Armor’s effect. So we didn’t know how many stages there really were.

But...

「Accumulated damage exceeded a threshold.」

「Effect triggered again.」

A double activation.

‘Is there another added effect at a higher stage?’ It was a reasonable guess, but I didn’t want to get my hopes up.

This alone was enough. ‘Aegis Dragon Armor’ deserves single-number ranking.

KRAAAASH! Time accelerated and the soul warrior’s greatsword charged at Harbae. And then—

“...La Abiestuka Bieran.”

Just as the chant finished, a dark crimson veil enveloped Harbae in a sphere.

The veil rippled ominously, far too sinister to be a protective shield.

‘I’ve never seen this before.’ It looked like one of Harbae’s hidden trump cards. I’d soon find out its power—

“...Huh?”

Watching carefully, my eyes went wide. I hadn’t expected this at all.

If the shield broke or the greatsword bounced off, that wouldn’t be surprising. But... whoooosh!

The greatsword disappeared as soon as it touched the veil. More precisely, it felt swallowed whole.

Like that sphere was a portal to another world. ‘Isn’t that basically invincibility...?’

Given the very short casting time, it seemed absurdly powerful.

Still, I decided not to feel unfair. Just like I scammed with Aegis Dragon Armor, this old man likely had a trump card too.

‘Besides, it probably isn’t no-cost.’ In our world, strong power always comes with a price. Like Harbae panting now.

“Hoo... hoo...”

Harbae breathed heavily as if using up all his future strength. His white hair was soaked with sweat, clinging to his skin. It looked like he could pass out any moment.

‘Have I ever seen him pushed this far before?’ I thought. It felt strangely out of place, but this was no time to just watch.

The veil had just disappeared. Thud! I quickened my steps and closed the distance to Harbae.

As I swung the hammer, another unexpected event happened. Whoosh! My hammer cut through empty air.

So absurd, I needed time to process it. ‘...He dodged?’

Harbae dodged my strike. No matter how low my agility, a mage shouldn’t be able to evade like that.

It wasn’t luck either. Whoosh! His movements dodging the follow-up attacks were as smooth as a martial arts form, enough to fluster me.

“You didn’t know, huh?”

“......”

“Magic developed in warfare. So ancient mages all trained in close combat. To protect themselves in melee.”

Damn it. If mages do martial arts too, what are we supposed to do for a living? Thinking that, I tried closing the gap more.

Step step. Instead of creating distance, Harbae approached me, reaching out. Then... thud. His hand lightly touched my stomach. Boom!

An explosive shock surged inside. No incantation or large magic flow was felt.

“Gahk!”

What the hell was that? Penetrating my magic resistance in Dragon Mode? ‘...That easily?’ I was stunned but quickly grasped the situation.

I had known this ‘technique’ but forgotten it over time.

[You might know this. You were temporarily untouchable. I connected the circuit, completing the spell through your body.]

[Ah, so that’s why your magic resistance didn’t work.] Though I don’t understand the exact principle.

The important part: Harbae ignored my magic resistance and dealt damage in close combat with Ibaekho.

“Okay, a mage in melee isn’t so easy after all.” Blood trickled down my throat, but it wasn’t a serious injury.

I judged I needed some time to regroup and created distance.

‘A bit disappointing not to have finished one on the first turn...’ But there was no rush. I’d at least incapacitated Leg Auress.

“Aures...!”

He seemed alive and likely to be healed soon.

‘I should have taken down at least one at the start.’ The situation wasn’t good. Ibaekho paused his attacks and spoke to me.

“You saw, right? No matter how much you struggle, it’s useless.”

He was cocky but it wasn’t over yet.

“So just stop here, yeah? Just say it. What did you talk about with that old man? You don’t have to tell anyone else, just me. Then we’ll end this, okay?”

I smiled at Ibaekho’s arrogance.

“Getting long-winded...”

“......?”

“Getting tired, huh?”

I wasn’t tired yet.

「Character cast [N O V E L I G H T] [Soul Dive].」

「Soul power regenerates proportional to soul consumed.」

Whether street fight, ring battle, jungle, ocean, or other worlds, one truth never changes.

“Behe—laaaaaaaah!!”

In the end, the one who stands till the last wins.*** One minute, two, three... The battle continued. Ten, twenty, thirty minutes... Rolling on the ground, coughing blood. One, two, three hours...

Fainting, waking in burning agony. Four hours. Maybe even longer.

Time passed slow like a teenager but also too fast.

“Huff... huff... huff...”

Hot breaths came from everywhere, not just me.

“Baron... maybe it’s enough now...?”

Leg Auress, who’d left and returned several times, guarded Harbae and pleaded earnestly.

“Even you have limits...”

Usually, the one who says that first is closest to their limit. Jaina added from afar.

“...Monster.”

A short murmur, as if thoroughly fed up.

“Ah ha... huh...”

Ibaekho, still sweating beads, wiped his brow and spoke.

“You definitely had some backup to believe in. Didn’t expect it to be yourself, though.”

He kept grinning like it was fun.

“Looks like it wasn’t that far off.”

Apparently, he liked how strong I’d become.

Chapter 700

“It's unfortunate that control has become harder... but our Baron has such clear weaknesses.”

Maybe I’m used to hearing this now. Even hearing that doesn’t really make me angry. Besides, it’s not like I hear it clearly. Thump thump thump thump—!

The pounding of my heart fills my eardrums. My vision blurred, soaked with blood and sweat. Skin burning as if roasted by fire.

“Krhhhhh...”

I laughed foolishly, focusing only on these sensations. It wasn’t laughter from amusement—it was just laughter that came out.

“That’s what Pebrosk wrote in his book. They say warriors laugh when they’re in pain...”

Parmel Harbae’s words made Ibaekho tilt his head.

“What kind of bullshit is that?”

“How would I know?” Ibaekho sneered, spitting blood-tinged saliva.

“Anyway, that means our Baron is dying from exhaustion, right?”

The resolve in Ibaekho’s eyes said he’d never back down. That was bad news for me.

Damn, I feel like I won’t even have the strength to stand soon...

“Krhhhhh...”

Well, what can I do? I’ll try as long as I can. Sometimes you need guts to charge forward, even knowing the outcome.

“Hey, old man. Ready?”

“Aah, it’s over.”

“Then let’s start wrapping this up.”

I didn’t know what preparation he meant, but it seemed he’d come up with some secret trump card without me knowing. Swish—!

A huge magic circle formed around Parmel Harbae where he stood. It was obviously serious.

For the first time in a long while, a faint weak feeling arose. ‘If I can’t block it, I better dodge immediately...’ I forced my eyes open and looked ahead.

Crack! Like a watermelon bursting, the magic circle on the ground lost its glow. What the hell was going on?

I quickly shifted my gaze and saw two things.

“...!”

Parmel Harbae flying through the air as if struck by something. And...

「Amelia Rainwails cast [Asura’s Fang].」

What? How is she here? Was I hallucinating from exhaustion? Just then Amelia spoke, looking at Ibaekho, not me.

“Leader, should we just kill all of these guys?”

I blankly nodded.

“Yeah.”

I nodded without realizing it, but it would be a lie to say I wasn’t surprised.

Amelia Rainwails. My real ally who should have been inside the walls. I was worried sick since she hadn’t returned and barely spoke for months...

While glad, I wondered how she got here. ‘Maybe she came looking for me because I didn’t come back...’

Yeah, if it was Amelia, she probably did. I trusted her more than anyone in that regard.

“Kill them? You alone against all of us?”

Ibaekho, pretending to be surprised by Amelia’s appearance, quickly regained composure and sneered.

“Aures! You protect the old man! I’ll handle this one!”

“Got it!”

Leg Auress ran toward Parmel Harbae on Ibaekho’s order. Amelia just smirked watching them.

“I don’t think I ever said that.”

“......”

“Alone.”

At that moment, everyone’s eyes turned toward the cave entrance hidden by the teleportation magic circle. Suddenly—! A flash of light shot from the dark cave.

It wasn’t a normal iron arrow, but light—specifically ‘Spirit Arrows’ fired with elemental power. Whoosh! No need to check who it was.

「Erwen Fornachi di Tersia cast [Burst].」

The arrow flew too fast to follow with the eyes, aimed at the groggy Parmel Harbae. Thud.

Aures rushed to shield the arrow desperately. Then—KA-BOOM!

The arrow exploded, tearing into the flesh of his left arm. Bits of flesh and blood scattered as if exploding from inside.

“So the elf’s here too?”

It was like time paused. Everyone stopped moving. Step by step, my allies slowly appeared from the cave entrance.

“Velbev Ruin Genesis...”

Erwen’s eyes burned with malice.

“Byorooooon!! Should we just wipe all these bastards out?”

Ainar was totally hyped up.

“Calm down. They’re not easy opponents.”

Bersil wore a tense expression... and Misha gave a worried look at the wounded Auyan. ‘Auyan’s here too.’ And the members didn’t stop there.

“That one’s nowhere to be seen. I heard there’s a skilled archer.”

“This is outside the walls...”

“But thank goodness. We found our missing leader as soon as we came out.”

“Yes. It seemed like an urgent situation too.”

Not just the founding members of Anabada Clan, but elite recent recruits like James Carra, Sven Parav, Meland Kaislan, and Lilis Marone who joined through the Ice Rock expedition.

‘Looking at it this way, our clan really has grown a lot...’

Enough in numbers to completely turn the tide. While a sense of relief bloomed deep inside, irritation filled Ibaekho’s eyes.

“...They came in a whole mob?”

I recognized it immediately. Experienced explorers do this naturally as breathing. Just as Ibaekho said before.

“Yulben Havelion.”

To confirm ‘that thing’ more clearly, I called out the opportunist’s name who stayed in the bystander position.

“...Yes?”

Maybe because he’s a mage, why does he look so dazed? The situation had changed 180 degrees.

“You’re just gonna stay there?”

“......”

“You might get hit and die.”

“Ah... yes...!”

Hearing Ibaekho’s exact words again, GM snapped to attention and rushed over. ‘This guy is really funny.’

He acted all innocent with Auril Gavis and clingy to Ibaekho before. Completely a sly fox. ‘Well, it’s natural for him.’

Actually, neither I nor Ibaekho are really GM’s allies.

To put it simply, GM clinging here and there doesn’t make me feel betrayed...

“...I’m sorry.”

“Enough, get lost already.”

“Yes...”

Ibaekho didn’t stop GM. Or rather, he didn’t dare to.

“Wow, with this lineup... we could probably go toe-to-toe with Orculus.”

He tried to sound casual, but I could ✪ Nоvеlіgһt ✪ (Official version) clearly see that Ibaekho was deeply unsettled by this unexpected turn.

‘Well, no wonder.’ Parmel Harbae, who was knocked away by Amelia’s kick, couldn’t even keep his balance.

“......”

Leg Auress had lost an arm. The healer Jaina barely had MP left...

“We need to heal them soon...”

“I’m fine. Don’t provoke them.”

“......”

Cornered like this, no one dared even think about healing.

“Hahaha... this is getting difficult...”

“......”

“Baron, shall we call it a draw...?”

What the hell is this crazy guy saying? I chuckled dumbfounded and lowered my gaze toward one of Ibaekho’s limbs.

“Neutering, right?”

“Um... Baron?”

“Well, with this hammer, we’ll manage somehow.”

“How about trying to resolve it by talking first...?”

Ignoring whatever she said, I stepped forward. Ibaekho clicked his tongue and sighed as if the earth might collapse.

“Hah... why does nothing ever go my way? How do they show up at this timing?”

Honestly, I’d be annoyed too. This was outside the walls, after all. I admit I was lucky. But...

‘Looking at him, it doesn’t seem like just luck.’ Watching Ibaekho’s team’s internal state, I thought—

If Ibaekho were in my situation, would any allies have come to look for him outside the walls? ‘Probably none.’

Thinking that, this moment no longer felt like mere luck. I couldn’t stress this enough.

“Byoooooorn!! When the hell is the fight starting!?”

Zero and one are different.*** Ibaekho’s team, now cornered. I don’t think I can take down Ibaekho here either.

‘If he decided to run, I’d never catch him.’ But still, this is a chance.

Leg Auress, Jaina Flyer, and the others Ibaekho raised and trained. Putting them aside for now.

“My sister’s enemy...”

I took another step, and Ibaekho grinned low and spoke.

“Haha, Baron? Stop coming closer, let’s talk for a bit.”

Step.

“No, I’m not listening. I’m not even curious anymore about what you did with that old man.”

Step.

“Oh, really... okay! Settlement money! Normally you settle with money at times like this, right? How much do you want? I’ll meet you halfway!”

Step.

“...Do we really have to see blood between us? Huh?”

Step. Ignoring him, I kept walking, and Ibaekho’s expression stiffened. Step. He no longer spoke with each step I took.

Step. Only when I stood before him, he muttered quietly, as if his previous posture was all a lie.

“Baron.”

“......”

“Are you really planning to go all the way with me?”

In his most familiar threatening tone, Ibaekho spoke.

“Whether you want to or not, the rest will die.”

No detailed explanation was given, but it was clear what he meant. [Star’s Demise], Ibaekho’s strongest ultimate.

He planned to use it like a modern nuclear weapon. A kind of chicken game?

If he took everyone down, I wouldn’t be able to handle it. I’m not alone here.

“......”

At a loss for words, Ibaekho curled his lips into a vicious smile.

“I knew it. What a weak heart you have. Doesn’t suit your looks.”

Ibaekho repeated this several times but my weak spot and trigger couldn’t be fixed. Step. He took a step toward me as he said it.

“Baron, I’ll give you just one piece of advice.”

I don’t know why everyone’s so eager to advise me.

“A man with something to lose can never beat me.”

Ibaekho looked me in the eyes honestly.

“I can give up everything.”

“......”

“For the one thing I desperately want.”

If it were someone else, I might have said something. But since it was Ibaekho, I couldn’t say a word. He would really do it.

Parmel Harbae, Jaina, Leg Auress — none of them would flinch if others died for their goals. So...

“......!”

Fearlessly stepping right in front of the powerhouse, I reached out and grabbed his collar. Squeezing tight—! Despite being grabbed, Ibaekho looked calm.

He seemed to truly believe I couldn’t hurt him here...

“Try.”

“......?”

“Try anything.”

Ibaekho frowned, not understanding my words. What’s there not to understand about what I just said? This guy clearly misunderstands me.

Losing something precious is naturally scary and painful. Just thinking about it squeezes your chest and drives you mad. But that’s why—CRACK!

I pulled his collar closer and headbutted him.

The chilling sound echoed far, but Ibaekho didn’t even groan, instead asking back.

“...What do you mean?”

Huh, does he really not get it? How dumb must he think I am? CRACK! Getting a little annoyed, I headbutted him again.

“...Are you seriously saying ‘try me’?”

No, I already said that before. CRACK!!! A much more chilling sound, blood dripping from Ibaekho’s forehead.

“Even if all your precious comrades die, you don’t care at all?”
